# Navier–Stokes formalization: faithfulness audit

> **Machine-generated and unconfirmed.** Agents wrote every verdict and finding below; no human has checked them. A PDE expert must review this report before any public use.

- Paper: *Finite time blowup for Navier–Stokes* (OpenAI), 166 pages, sha256 `0e779481c4da40bd28d1e642e1d8ca57447d129610df28dfa5a11e9af8ae228f`, <https://cdn.openai.com/pdf/32d9f210-8b73-45e0-91bc-82a30aef8a9a/navier-stokes.pdf>, retrieved 2026-10-09.

The 6 least confident OK verdicts were re-audited blind; 6 agreed. Findings the pass touched carry a Review line.

## Main theorems against Clay (C) and (D)

Three independent checks of `NavierStokes.Comparator.navier_stokes_breakdown_R3` and `…_periodic` against Fefferman's official problem statement.

### Definitions (`clay:definitions`)

Verdict: **confirmed**, confidence 0.88.

Yes. Both Lean theorems state (C) and (D) faithfully. For (D) this means the version with the official errata: the errata asks for a periodic pressure. I checked every clause in both directions: - **Data:** the Lean conditions on u₀ and f imply Clay's smooth/divergence-free requirement and (4), (5), (8), (9). - **Solutions:** every Clay solution, extended arbitrarily to t<0, is a Lean solution. No clause looks at values for t<0. So the Lean "no solution" claim is at least as strong as Clay's. What I confirmed directly: - (1), (2), (3) are encoded correctly, using Mathlib's Laplacian and gradient. - `ContDiffOn ℝ ∞` on univ ×ˢ Ici 0 means smooth up to t=0. ∞ is (⊤:ℕ∞), so this is C^∞ and not analytic. - (7) has an L² (`MemLp`) clause, so a non-integrable field cannot slip through with a Bochner integral of 0. - The pressure on ℝ³ is unconstrained, exactly as in Clay. - The class is not empty: v=p=f=0 is a valid solution. The remaining differences are conventional: - ν is quantified as ∀ν>0, with witnesses allowed to depend on ν. Rescaling makes this equivalent to "some ν > 0". - The multi-index bounds are written as Fréchet-norm bounds, with K real. - The time derivative is one-sided at t=0. - The periodic pressure condition is taken from the errata. ComparatorDefinitions.lean has the same definition text as the challenge file, and both are byte-identical to upstream formal-conjectures 8bf45ed apart from the namespace and notation.

- **low CONTENT**: Periodic solution class requires the pressure to be 1-periodic (from the errata). This is narrower than the main text of (D), so Lean (D) excludes only periodic-pressure solutions. It matches the official errata on page 6. Source: “The further condition p(x + ej , t) = p(x, t) should be made explicit in Eqn (8).” Consequence: Matches the errata-corrected (D), so the relation is equivalent. Against (D) without the errata it is lean_weaker. A solution whose pressure is q + c(t)·x corresponds to a periodic-pressure solution with the time-translated force f(x+Y(t),t), so such solutions are not excluded. The errata puts the condition in (8), a data condition; Lean treats it as a constraint on the unknown p, which is the only coherent reading.
- **low CONTENT**: ν is quantified universally: ∀ ν>0, ∃ (u₀,f), and the witnesses may depend on ν. This matches the natural reading of 'Take ν > 0'. It is equivalent to the 'some ν > 0' reading by parabolic scaling. Source: “Take ν > 0 and n = 3. Then there exist a smooth, divergence-free vector field u◦(x) on R3” Consequence: No defect; the Lean form is equivalent to or stronger than either reading. Under ũ(x,t)=c·u(x,ct), p̃=c²p(x,ct), f̃=c²f(x,ct), viscosity ν becomes cν and (4)-(11) are preserved. The repo uses the same rescaling (rescaledForce).
- **low CONTENT**: (4), (5), (9) bound each multi-index derivative ∂_x^α ∂_t^m. Lean instead bounds the operator norm of the total m-th Fréchet derivative, jointly in (x,t) with the max product norm, for every real K. The two are equivalent up to constants. Source: “\|∂xα u◦(x)\| ≤ CαK (1 + \|x\|)−K on Rn, for any α and K” Consequence: Different formulation, equivalent. \|∂^α_x ∂^k_t f\| = \|D^{\|α\|+k}f[(e_i,0)…,(0,1)…]\| ≤ ‖D^{\|α\|+k}f‖, and ‖D^N f‖ ≤ (n+1)^N · max over partials of order N. Real K, including negative values, adds nothing beyond K≥0 because 1+\|x\|+t ≥ 1.
- **low CONTENT**: Fields are typed on all of ℝ³×ℝ. The time derivative is the one-sided derivWithin on Ici 0, smoothness is ContDiffOn on univ ×ˢ Ici 0, and periodicity of f, v, p is required only for t≥0. Values at t<0 are unconstrained. Source: “p, u ∈ C∞(Rn × [0, ∞))” Consequence: Equivalent. No clause reads values at t<0, so restricting to t≥0 or extending arbitrarily gives a bijection with Clay's objects on ℝ³×[0,∞). One-sided differentiation at t=0 is the standard meaning of C^∞ up to the boundary, and univ ×ˢ Ici 0 is UniqueDiffOn.
- **low CONTENT**: Bounded energy (7) is encoded as MemLp (‖v·t‖) 2 for each t≥0, plus ∃E, ∀t≥0, a Bochner integral of ‖v x t‖² below E. The MemLp clause rules out the Bochner junk value 0 for non-integrable fields. Source: “\|u(x, t)\|2 dx < C for all t ≥ 0 (bounded energy)” Consequence: Equivalent. For a smooth u, a finite Lebesgue integral means L², and v(·,t) is continuous, hence measurable. With MemLp the Bochner and Lebesgue integrals agree, and C is uniform in t in both.

Verdict: **confirmed**, confidence 0.87.

I diffed ComparatorChallenges/NavierStokes.lean against the upstream file it cites (formal-conjectures at 8bf45ed, fetched with WebFetch). Every definition and both theorem statements match upstream token for token. The only differences are the import, the inlined ℝ^n/ℝ³ notation (same right-hand side as upstream), the namespace, ∇⬝ becoming local notation, dropped category/AMS attributes, dropped (A)/(B), and docstrings. I checked the upstream encoding against Fefferman's p.1–2 conditions (1)–(11): ∀ν>0, the decay conditions (4)/(5)/(9), smoothness on ℝ³×[0,∞), the MemLp field plus the energy bound for (7), periodicity (8)/(10), and the one-sided time derivative at t=0. ### Against the paper's claims (`clay:paper`)

Verdict: **confirmed**, confidence 0.85.

Yes. Both theorems state Clay (C) and (D) faithfully, (D) in the form fixed by the official erratum. The quantifier order is ∀ν>0 ∃(u₀,f) ¬∃(v,p): ν is fixed first and the data may depend on it. That matches "Take ν > 0 … Then there exist" in Clay, "For every ν > 0 there exist a force" in the paper, and "For every positive viscosity" in the README. Everything else is equivalent: (1)–(7) and (8)–(11), with C^∞ meaning ∞, not ω. Smoothness makes every derivative genuine, so junk values give no loophole. The solution file's statements match the reference text exactly. ComparatorDefinitions.lean matches the reference file line for line, minus the two theorems. The reference matches upstream formal-conjectures 8bf45ed, apart from namespace, notation, attributes and the removal of (A)/(B). I did not run Comparator, so this is not a kernel-level check. Remaining differences are all low severity: - (D) excludes only solutions whose pressure is periodic. This follows the erratum and is weaker than the unamended text. - The main theorems keep only the nonexistence sentence of Thm 1.1 and Cor 10.6. The blow-up construction is stated elsewhere, with project-internal definitions. - The README's torus bullet leaves out the periodicity requirement on the solution. - The decay and energy conditions are encoded by routine reformulations.

- **low CONTENT**: (D) excludes only solutions whose pressure is also 1-periodic, following the official erratum. Measured against the unamended text of (D) (velocity periodic, pressure only smooth), the Lean statement is weaker. Source: “The further condition p(x + ej, t) = p(x, t) should be made explicit in Eqn (8). (Clay errata, p.6)” Consequence: This matches the official statement, erratum included. The paper also relies on it: p.125 says 'as required in the erratum', and the uniqueness step on p.126 integrates the pressure term away by periodicity. Without the erratum, (D) would be a stronger claim that neither the paper nor the Lean proves.
- **low CONTENT**: The Comparator theorems keep only the nonexistence sentence of Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 10.6, quantified over the data. They drop the zero datum, the compactly supported force, the explicit blow-up solution on [0,1), its bounded energy and its L∞ blow-up. Relation: partial. Source: “For every ν > 0 there exist a force f ∈ C_c^∞(R³ × (0,∞); R³), a compact set K ⊂ R³, and smooth velocity and …” Consequence: Comparator's independent check covers (C) and (D) only. The paper's headline construction (abstract, Thm 1.1) is checked only against the project's own definitions. This does not affect the Clay claim.
- **low CONTENT**: The README's torus bullet says 'no global smooth solution exists'. The Lean excludes only solutions that are spatially periodic in velocity and pressure. The whole-space bullet leaves out the decay conditions (4)/(5), which the Lean does impose; that only makes the Lean stronger. Source: “for which there exist no solutions (p,u) of (1), (2), (3), (10), (11) on R³ × [0,∞). (Clay (D), p.2)” Consequence: Imprecise wording only. The README's appeal to '(D)' is accurate, because (D) restricts the competitors via (10) and the erratum.
- **low CONTENT**: Quantifier order is ∀ν>0 ∃(u₀,f): ν is fixed first and the data depend on it. This matches Clay, the paper and the README. It is stronger than an existential reading of 'Take ν > 0', and the two readings are equivalent by viscosity scaling. The data are not uniform in ν, which Clay does not ask for. Source: “Take ν > 0 and n = 3. Then there exist a smooth, divergence-free vector field u°(x) on R³ and a smooth f(x,t)” Consequence: Not a defect: the ∀ν form implies every reading of (C)/(D). The rescaling u^ν(x,t)=νu(x,νt), p^ν=ν²p(x,νt), f^ν=ν²f(x,νt) makes all ν equivalent anyway.
- **low CONTENT**: These encodings are routine reformulations of Clay's conditions: - (4)/(5)/(9) use the norm of the total-order Fréchet derivative, with a real exponent K, instead of mixed partials. - (7) adds MemLp at every time. - (1) uses derivWithin on [0,∞) and also holds at t=0. - (6)/(11) use ContDiffOn on univ ×ˢ Ici 0. All are equivalent for smooth fields. Smoothness also makes the solution class genuinely classical, so junk values give no loophole. Source: “\|∂_x^α ∂_t^m f(x,t)\| ≤ C_αmK (1 + \|x\| + t)^−K on R^n × [0,∞), for any α, m, K.” Consequence: different_formulation with no change of meaning: in finite dimensions, mixed partials and total-derivative norms bound each other up to constants. The statements are not weakened or strengthened.

## The preprint's two claims

arXiv:2610.08144 (Bastounis, Circelli, Hansen) claims two mistranslations. Each was checked twice: once to verify it, once for its consequences.

### Claim 1, Lemma 8.6 (8.19): verification (`claim1:verification`)

Verdict: **confirmed**, confidence 0.90.

The claim checks out. (8.19) on p.95 says ‖N^{-1}F‖_{C^m} ≤ C_m‖F‖_{C^{m+4}} for v_t=(√2−1,1), and its proof on p.96 uses \|v_t·k\|^{-1} ≤ C(1+\|k\|) plus a summable (1+\|k\|)^{-3} majorant. The Lean declarations exist at the cited lines and every one needs m+5: norm_derivativeWord_inverse_le (L1059), inverse_finiteJets (L865), both mixedJet_inverse_bound (PTI L654, SFTI L1082), and the derived realInverse_finiteJets and familyInverse_finiteJets(_fiber). The extra derivative comes from the lattice majorant Σ weight^{-4}, built as a product of two 1-D Σ(1+\|n\|)^{-2} sums. Σ weight^{-3} is also summable, so m+4 was within reach. I found no declaration that states the m+4 bound. Relation: lean_weaker by one derivative. The difference does not matter downstream. The paper only needs a fixed finite loss (p.64, p.96), and the class M_α (Def 6.4, p.69) is defined by bounds at every derivative order. Lean's MemClass.bounds is likewise ∀ m, and meanClass_temporalInverse absorbs the loss by calling hclass.bounds (m+5). The main theorems do not depend on the m+4 count. norm_derivativeWord_inverse_le and both mixedJet_inverse_bound are unused leaves. The bound that carries weight is inverse_finiteJets.

- **low CONTENT**: Every Lean torus-inverse estimate needs m+5 input derivatives where (8.19) needs m+4. No declaration states the m+4 version. The cause is the cruder lattice majorant Σ weight^{-4}. The difference is harmless because all downstream classes are all-order. Source: “‖N^{-1}F‖_{C^m_y} ≤ C_m ‖F‖_{C^{m+4}_y} (8.19); 'Four more derivatives of F than the requested output leave a summable (1+\|k\|)^{-3} bound'” Consequence: The paper's (8.19) is not formalized as written; Lean proves a version weaker by one derivative. The paper's later uses (p.96 'class estimates at every fixed derivative order retain their ε exponent'; p.64 'only a finite loss of torus derivatives') need only a fixed finite loss, and Lean's MeanClass/MemClass.bounds is ∀ m, so meanClass_temporalInverse(_moving) absorb the loss via hclass.bounds (m + 5). No effect on the main theorems.

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- Paper p.95 (img p-095.png): Lemma 8.6 with N = v_t·∂_y, eq. (8.19): N^{-1}F = Σ_{k≠0} F̂(k)/(2πi v_t·k) e^{2πik·y}, ‖N^{-1}F‖_{C^m_y} ≤ C_m‖F‖_{C^{m+4}_y}.
- Paper p.96 proof: divisor bound (6.7), 'Four more derivatives of F than the requested output leave a summable (1+\|k\|)^{-3} bound'. The later use: 'four additional derivatives in the torus variables suffice, so the class estimates at every fixed derivative order retain their ε exponent'.
- Paper p.64: inverse directional operators 'have only a finite loss of torus derivatives'. p.69, Def 6.4 (6.24): M_α bounds hold for every multi-index I, with C_{j,I}, b_{j,I}, d_{j,I} allowed to depend on I.
- TorusInverse.lean:195-197: vector .temporal = (√2 − 1, 1), which is v_t. multiplier :248 = (omega*symbol)^{-1} with omega = 2πi. norm_multiplier_le :260 gives ≤ 6‖ω^{-1}‖·weight k. inverse_derivativeWord_bound :503 bounds a word by coeffSeminorm (\|w\|+1).
- SmoothFourierData.lean:368-387, coefficient_seminorm_bound: coeffSeminorm p ≤ 3^{p+4}C Σ weight^{-4}. ### Claim 1: consequences (`claim1:consequences`)

Verdict: **confirmed**, confidence 0.90.

The claim is accurate, but the gap it finds does no harm. Lemma 8.6, eq. (8.19) (p95) states ‖N⁻¹F‖_{C^m} ≤ C_m‖F‖_{C^{m+4}}. The paper's proof on p96 is sound: \|v_t·k\|⁻¹ ≲ 1+\|k\|, and Σ(1+\|k\|)⁻³ converges in 2D. The Lean declarations sit at the cited lines and need m+5. The extra derivative comes from the proof method. SmoothFourierData.coefficient_seminorm_bound uses the lattice majorant Σweight⁻⁴, the product of two 1D sums, instead of Σweight⁻³. No Lean declaration states the inverse with loss m+4. The bound is used through inverse_finiteJets and realInverse_finiteJets. These feed the temporal-update M_α class lemma (VariableGaugeMean.meanClass_temporalInverse_moving L2463), which sits on the main-theorem path, and the Lemma 8.2 alias bounds. Every consumer takes the class bounds at order m+5 (`hclass.bounds (m + 5)`), and MemClass carries bounds at every order. The paper also needs only some fixed finite loss: Definition 6.4 sets bounds at every multi-index. The m+5 version is therefore enough everywhere it is used, and neither main theorem is affected. The per-word lemma norm_derivativeWord_inverse_le and both mixedJet_inverse_bound declarations are not used anywhere, except that one feeds the other.

- **low CONTENT**: The torus-inverse estimate is proved with five lost derivatives, not four. Every Lean version (per word, full-jet, real, family) has m+5 where (8.19) has m+4. So (8.19) is not formalized as stated, only a version weaker by one derivative. Source: “‖N^{-1}F‖_{C^m_y} ≤ C_m ‖F‖_{C^{m+4}_y} (8.19), p95” Consequence: The Lean does not prove (8.19) as written, so the paper's sharper statement was not checked. The difference has no downstream effect. The paper uses only that the loss is finite: "only a finite loss of torus derivatives" (p64), and M_α holds at every order. Every Lean consumer reads the class bounds at order m+5. The main theorems are unaffected.
- **low CONTENT**: The same +5 loss carries into the bounds that rest on inverse_finiteJets. The radial alias of Lemma 8.2 is iterated with 5 torus derivatives plus 1 radial derivative per step, giving m+6p. Paper (8.10) states m+p+3. It is harmless for the same reason. Source: “‖L^{-p}H‖_{C^m_y} ≤ C_{m,p}‖H‖_{C^{m+p+3}_y}, p ≥ 1. (8.10), p91” Consequence: This is outside Claim 1's cited declarations but has the same cause. Losses are absorbed by the all-order class bounds and alias decay, so the main results are unaffected.

- Paper p95 (image p-095.png): Lemma 8.6, (8.19) gives the Fourier formula Σ_{k≠0} F̂(k)/(2πi v_t·k) e^{2πik·y} and the bound C^{m+4} → C^m.
- Paper p96 proof: the paper says four more derivatives of F leave 'a summable (1 + \|k\|)^{-3} bound'. That is correct, since Σ_{Z²}(1+\|k\|)^{-3} < ∞. Later use (p96): 'four additional derivatives in the torus variables suffice, so the class estimates at every fixed derivative order retain their ε exponent'. Only finiteness of the loss is used.
- Paper p69, Definition 6.4: M_α is defined by ∀I ∈ N_0^5 ∃C_{j,I}, b_{j,I}, d_{j,I}, i.e. bounds at every order. p64: 'only a finite loss of torus derivatives'. p91 (8.10): the radial analogue, with loss m+p+3.
- TorusInverse.lean:195-197: vector .temporal = (√2−1, 1) = v_t. L248: multiplier = (ω·symbol)⁻¹ with ω = 2πi, which matches the (8.19) series. L503: inverse_derivativeWord_bound gives ‖D^w N⁻¹a‖ ≤ (6‖ω⁻¹‖‖ω‖^{\|w\|})·coeffSeminorm(\|w\|+1) a.

L325/343 obtain the weight^{-4} majorant from a product of 1D (1+\|n\|)^{-2} sums, and this is where the extra derivative comes from.

L865 inverse_finiteJets = applyMultiplier_finiteJets n 1, giving n+1+4. L1020 nonbarPart_finiteJets has n+4, but it is the centering multiplier, not the inverse.

### Claim 2, (10.19) in Lemma 10.5: verification (`claim2:verification`)

Verdict: **partly_confirmed**, confidence 0.88.

The declarations exist at the cited lines and say what the preprint reports. One correction: the R3StressPressureEstimate name is `regularized_flux_bound`, lowercase. No Lean declaration states (10.19) as the paper writes it. The Lean proves a different estimate by a different route, and neither inequality implies the other. Several of the preprint's listed differences are overstated. The commutator term R^{-7/4}B^{3/4} is exactly the paper's C_T R^{-1}·R^{-3/4}B^{3/4}, and it uses the same L^{4/3}/L^4 pairing and the same L^4 interpolation. The changes are confined to the local term: - L^{6/5}×L^6 instead of L^{3/2}×L^3; - Ḣ¹→L⁶ Sobolev plus L² Riesz bounds instead of L^{3/2} Calderón–Zygmund; - a φ² multiplier instead of φ⁴; - an A_R/R term and a 1/R² term. The sign difference (w = u−v) cancels inside the flux integrand. The Lean bound is sufficient for its only use. There, every power of A_R is at most 3/2, which is the property the paper's Young/Gronwall step needs. The Lean Lemma 10.5 (candidate_unique_on_Icc) has the paper's conclusion v = u on [0,T] and is fully proved with no sorry. So the claim that the statement is mistranslated is refuted for Lemma 10.5 itself. The main theorems are unaffected.

- **low CONTENT**: No declaration states (10.19) itself. The Lean bound contains A_R (A_R/R term), adds a 1/R² term and lacks the B_R^{3/2}/R and B_R^{1/2}/R shape. Neither bound implies the other. The commutator term is identical to the paper's. Proof-internal only. Source: “\|∫ π w·∇χ_R\| ≤ C_T R^{-1}[(B_R+1)B_R^{1/2} + R^{-3/4}B_R^{3/4}]. (10.19)” Consequence: Lean checked a different, valid estimate, not the paper's argument as written. Lemma 10.5 and the main theorems are unaffected, because the only consumer (exists_uniform_flux_absorption, which absorbs every term into δA²+D/R) is satisfied by the Lean form.

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- Paper p.123 (img p-123.png): (10.19) bounds \|∫πw·∇χ_R\| by C_T R^{-1}[(B_R+1)B_R^{1/2}+R^{-3/4}B_R^{3/4}]. It follows the identity ∫πw·∇χ_R = ∫π_*w·∇χ_R, which holds 'for almost every t'. The paper then says 'every power of A_R in these flux bounds is at most 3/2 < 2' and Young gives C_T/R.
- Paper p.122: the paper splits φ_R^4 π_* = ΣR_iR_j(φ^4 g) + Σ[φ^4,R_iR_j]g. The first sum is bounded in L^{3/2} by C_T(B_R+1), using L^p Riesz boundedness [20] and \|\|u\|\|_6. The commutator is bounded in L^{4/3} by C_T R^{-3/4}.
- The definitions match the paper: ComparisonCutoffs.cutoff/weight/multiplier (NavierStokes/R3/ComparisonCutoffs.lean:32,35,38) are φ_R, χ_R = φ_R^8, and a φ_R^2 multiplier (the paper uses φ_R^4). Comparison.dissipationRoot (ComparisonSetup.lean:47) is A_R. Comparison.cutoffL6 (:50) is B_R = \|\|φ^4 w\|\|_6. tensorDiff (:53) is u_iu_j − v_iv_j, the opposite sign of the paper's g.
- Roles: in WholeSpaceUniqueness.classical_uniqueness_on_Icc (NavierStokes/R3/WholeSpaceUniqueness.lean:30), u is the compact reference and v the competitor. So (p−q)·Dχ_R[u−v] = (−π)(−w)·∇χ_R = π w·∇χ_R, and the integrand is identical to the paper's.
- Local term: PressureFlux.localized_pair_bound (PressureFlux.lean:316) pairs L^{6/5} with L^6. It uses LocalizedTensorBounds.weighted_tensorDiff_bound (LocalizedTensorBounds.lean:170): \|\|φ²g\|\|_{6/5} ≤ M^{3/2}B^{1/2}+2M\|\|u\|\|_3. It also uses riesz_rTest_bound (:289) via RieszTestOperators.lpNorm_six_rieszTest_le (RieszTestOperators.lean:223), which is GNS Sobolev Ḣ¹→L⁶ plus an L² bound on ∂R_iR_jψ. Together these give C(A/R+M/R²).
- Commutator term: cutoff_commutator_bound (PressureFlux.lean:463) uses Comparison.riesz_commutator_pair_bound (HeatKernelCommutator.lean:97), with R^{-3/4}\|\|g\|\|_1\|\|ψ\|\|_4, and PressureFluxTest.cutoffTest_four_bound (PressureFluxTest.lean:309), with \|\|8φ^5∇φ·w\|\|_4 ≤ (C/R)M^{1/4}B^{3/4}. The result is R^{-7/4}B^{3/4}, the same as the paper's R^{-1}·R^{-3/4}.
- R3StressPressureEstimate.lean:38 is `regularized_flux_bound` (lowercase b). It is a per-component bound for the Gaussian-regularized stress, rawBound with L² stress × L² flux factor. rawBound_le (:92) gives coefficient/R·(B^{3/2}+B+1). That is close to the paper's shape but not exact. Nothing imports this module, and it is not reachable from NavierStokes/ComparatorSolution.lean (import-graph traversal, 609 modules). NavierStokes.R3PressureFlux, R3EnergyAbsorption, R3EnergyNorms and R3PhysicalPressureBound are also unreachable.

- Only use: WholeSpaceUniqueness.lean:60 obtains CP and feeds WholeSpaceComparisonClosure.eq_of_pressure_flux_bound (WholeSpaceComparisonClosure.lean:32; pressureEnvelope :25). That goes to ComparisonRateBound.exists_uniform_rate_bound (ComparisonRateBound.lean:127) and then exists_uniform_flux_absorption (:31). Given B ≤ S(A+M/R), the absorption step proves that the C1/R·B^{3/2} term plus the Lean envelope is ≤ δA²+D/R. Gronwall follows in WholeSpaceEnergyLimit.eq_zero_of_weighted_rate_bound (WholeSpaceEnergyLimit.lean:70).

WholeSpaceUniqueness.candidate_unique_on_Icc (:72) has hypotheses T<1, v,q smooth on [0,T]×R³, UniformFiniteEnergy (Icc 0 T) v (= L∞L²), div v=0, NS at ν=1 with force f, and v(0)=0. It concludes ∀ t∈[0,T], ∀ x, u=v, and it holds for any CandidateProperties candidate, so it matches or generalizes the paper's lemma. ### Claim 2: consequences (`claim2:consequences`)

Verdict: **partly_confirmed**, confidence 0.88.

The cited declarations exist at the stated lines: PressureFlux.lean L516, L546 and L576. The fourth is named regularized_flux_bound (lowercase b), at R3StressPressureEstimate.lean L38. The L576 bound reads exactly as the preprint quotes it, and it does differ from (10.19). It has A_R/R and R^{-2} terms where the paper has R^{-1}(B+1)B^{1/2}. The two bounds are incomparable. The proof also takes another route: L^{6/5}×L^6 duality, Sobolev and L² Riesz bounds, a φ² commutator and ‖u‖₃. The paper uses L^{3/2} Calderón–Zygmund bounds, a φ⁴ commutator and ‖u‖₆. Some of the preprint's points are overstated. The commutator term R^{-7/4}B^{3/4} is the paper's R^{-1}·R^{-3/4}B^{3/4}, the same in both. The sign flips cancel, and both proofs interpolate. The Lean also holds for every t in (0,T), where the paper has a.e. t. No declaration states (10.19) in the paper's form. Its only use is in eq_of_pressure_flux_bound, through classical_uniqueness_on_Icc, and there exists_uniform_flux_absorption absorbs it into δA²+D/R, which is all the paper's Young/Gronwall step needs. candidate_unique_on_Icc concludes u=v on [0,T], matching Lemma 10.5. The main theorem is unaffected. The file R3StressPressureEstimate is an orphan module that no file imports. Calling this a "mistranslation" is an overstatement: it is a different, valid estimate used at an internal proof step.

- **medium CONTENT**: The Lean flux bound is not the paper's (10.19). Its local term (B^{1/2}+1)(A_R/R+R^{-2}) replaces R^{-1}(B+1)B^{1/2}. The two are incomparable, even after using B≲A+M/R from (10.17). The commutator terms R^{-7/4}B^{3/4} agree. Source: “\|∫ π w·∇χ_R\| ≤ C_T R^{-1}[(B_R+1)B_R^{1/2} + R^{-3/4}B_R^{3/4}] (10.19)” Consequence: The intermediate inequality checked is not the paper's. Both bounds are absorbable, though: every power of A is below 2 once B≤S(A+M/R). Lemma 10.5 and the main theorem are unaffected.
- **low CONTENT**: The proof route differs. Lean pairs φ²g in L^{6/5} with R_iR_j applied to the test function in L^6, using Sobolev and L² Riesz bounds. It commutes with φ² rather than φ⁴, and uses ‖u‖₃ rather than ‖u‖₆. The paper's L^{3/2} Calderón–Zygmund step is never formalized. Source: “We use the standard L^p boundedness of Riesz transforms for 1 < p < ∞” Consequence: The paper's argument for (10.19), as written, was not checked. A valid alternative argument was formalized and fully proved, with no sorry in these files.
- **low CONTENT**: The sign conventions are reversed: Lean takes w=u−v, π=p−q and tensorDiff=u_iu_j−v_iv_j=−g_ij, with u the reference solution. The flux integrand πw·∇χ_R is unchanged, so this is cosmetic. Source: “w = v − u, π = P − p, g_ij = v_iv_j − u_iu_j” Consequence: None.
- **low COVERAGE**: No declaration states (10.19) in the paper's form, (B+1)B^{1/2}/R + R^{-7/4}B^{3/4}. Only its ingredients exist: the R^{-3/4} kernel bound and the L^4 interpolation. The orphan R3Pressure* cluster uses yet another bound. Source: “Consequently \|∫ π w·∇χ_R\| ≤ C_T R^{-1}[(B_R+1)B_R^{1/2} + R^{-3/4}B_R^{3/4}]” Consequence: (10.19) is a display inside a proof, not a numbered item. Its role is filled by the Lean variant.

- Paper p.123 (img p-123.png): (10.19) holds for a.e. t, follows 'for almost every t ∈ (0,T)', and the paper factors out C_T R^{-1}. Paper p.122 defines A_R, B_R and χ_R=φ_R^8, (10.17) B_R ≤ C(A_R+R^{-1}‖w‖₂), and (10.18) commutes with φ_R^4. The commutator has L^{4/3} norm ≤ C_T R^{-3/4}.
- Paper p.121 Lemma 10.5: 'then v = u on that interval'. Its hypotheses are smoothness on [0,T]×R³, UniformFiniteEnergy (Icc 0 T) v, div and NS on Ioo 0 T, zero datum and T<1. The statements match, and the Lean is slightly more general.

## Confirmed findings

### Medium (6)

<a id="f1"></a>**F1. Lemma 4.4: COVERAGE (medium)**, confidence 0.87

- No declaration stating part (i) as a whole was found. Generic lemmas state its steps separately: 𝔪₁=𝔪₂ (NominalProfile.moments_propagate), a, b_s, V₀ (NaturalCoefficientBridge.radialFlux_congr_germ) and, for axis-regular profiles, Π, p_s, T₀. Beyond a join, the only combination found is at the modulation-repair site (LeadingStressWeights.stress_outside), without V₀.
- Paper: “Then m1 = m2, and the corresponding quantities Πi, V0,i, Qs,i, Ns,i, ps,i, ai, bs,i, T0,i agree for all X ≥ Xh”
- Lean: NavierStokes.NominalProfile.moments_propagate (NavierStokes/NominalProfile.lean:486): For arbitrary fields U,E,V,G and a ≤ r: five densities integrable on (0,r], moments equal at a, and (U,E)=(V,G) at (x,η) for all x>a imply moments U E r η = moments V G r η (all five rows of (4.15)).; NavierStokes.NaturalCoefficientBridge.radialFlux_congr_germ (NavierStokes/NaturalCoefficientBridge.lean:280): If U = V and average U = average V near w, then radialFlux h lam U = radialFlux h lam V near w. Generic; needs a full neighbourhood, so it does not give X = X_h itself.; NavierStokes.SlowDivergence.radialFlux (NavierStokes/SlowDivergence.lean:21): radialFlux h lam U p = X/L · (2ηU − 2η(D+lam)·average U − d·∂_η(average U)); at lam = 0 this is the paper's V₀ of (4.7). So V₀ does have a Lean counterpart.; NavierStokes.LeadingStressWeights.stress_outside (NavierStokes/LeadingStressWeights.lean:98): For a modulated witness v over nominal W: 0 < X, η ∈ [-1,1], and X < modulation.left or repairPatch.right < X (strict) imply stress v.profiles h p = stress W.profiles h p. Proof combines fields_outside, stocks_outside, shears_outside, stress_eq_of_coordinates.; NavierStokes.LeadingStressWeights.stress_eq_of_coordinates (NavierStokes/LeadingStressWeights.lean:84): For Profiles P, Q at a point with X > 0, Q.f ≠ 0: equal f, shearA, shearB, p1, p2 imply stress P h p = stress Q h p (the T₀ step, pointwise).; NavierStokes.ModulatedExterior.pressure_equal_after (NavierStokes/ModulatedExterior.lean:61): For Profiles P, Q with common pressure0, equal ∫f² at (B,η) and f equal on [B,X] at η: P.pressure (X,η) = Q.pressure (X,η). Requires (r,η) in both domains for all r ∈ [0,X], including the axis.; NavierStokes.ModulatedExterior.integral_equal_after (NavierStokes/ModulatedExterior.lean:28): For real functions f, g continuous on [0,X], 0 ≤ B ≤ X: ∫₀ᴮ f = ∫₀ᴮ g and f = g on [B,X] imply ∫₀ˣ f = ∫₀ˣ g (one row).; NavierStokes.ActivationStocks.profiles_stocks_congr (NavierStokes/ActivationStocks.lean:833): For Profiles P, Q at (X,η), X > 0, η in an open set J: f, U equal at the point and all five histories equal at (X,ξ) for ξ ∈ J imply profileStockOne and profileStockTwo (p_s) equal at the point.; NavierStokes.ModulatedCone.shears_eq_of_radial_eventuallyEq (NavierStokes/ModulatedCone.lean:514): For arbitrary functions: E = F and U = V on a two-sided radial neighbourhood of p.1 imply angularShear and signedAxialShear (a, −b_s) agree at p. Does not cover X = X_h under tail agreement.; NavierStokes.ModulatedProfileAssembly.Witness.stocks_outside (NavierStokes/ModulatedProfileAssembly.lean:627): Site instance: outside the open modulation/repair interval the modulated profile's two stocks equal the nominal ones; applies profiles_stocks_congr with rows from rows_outside (witness field restored).; NavierStokes.ModulatedProfileAssembly.Witness.shears_outside (NavierStokes/ModulatedProfileAssembly.lean:641): Site instance: for X < modulation.left or X > repairPatch.right (strict), the modulated profile's shears equal the nominal ones; applies shears_eq_of_radial_eventuallyEq.; NavierStokes.ModulatedHistories.actual_histories_restored (NavierStokes/ModulatedHistories.lean:988): Site-specific: after the repair patch (X ≥ P.right) the repaired, localized profile's five axis histories equal the original ones; proved by a support argument, not by moments_propagate.; NavierStokes.NominalProfile.joined_moments (NavierStokes/NominalProfile.lean:515): For r ≥ matchFraction, moments of the joined pair equal moments of F.U, F.E; the proof applies moments_propagate (call at :530).; NavierStokes.NominalProfile.Controls.heated_moments (NavierStokes/NominalProfile.lean:2012): For r ≥ heatJoin, moments of (c.U, heatedE) equal those of the heated outgoing pair; the proof is a direct application of moments_propagate (:2017).; NavierStokes.NominalConeAssembly.coordinates_of_prefix (NavierStokes/NominalConeAssembly.lean:122): Generic bundle for Profiles P, Q with common pressure0 agreeing for X ≤ R (prefix, not tail): shearA, shearB, p1, p2 agree at points with 0 < X ≤ R.; NavierStokes.ProfileHistories.Profiles (NavierStokes/ProfileHistories.lean:303): Structure with f, U smooth on a RadialDomain (:31), an open set containing every radial segment down to the axis; E := √(2X)·f. So f = E/√(2X) must be smooth through X = 0.
- Consequence: The general joining identity is not checked as one theorem. The nominal and heat joins apply moments_propagate; the modulation repair combines the step lemmas for its own profile, strictly outside the modulated interval. The Π, p_s and T₀ lemmas require profiles regular at the axis; the paper's lemma does not. The paper's uses of 4.4(i) were not traced site by site. The main theorems use no such lemma.
- Dissent: Both skeptics upheld. The MATHEMATICS skeptic noted that 'only one-row fragments' was inaccurate because moments_propagate covers all five rows generically. The summary now reflects that, and severity and ledger are unchanged.
- Review: Wording corrected after an independent checker called it overstated and an adjudicator re-read the cited declarations.

<a id="f2"></a>**F2. Theorem 4.6: COVERAGE (medium)**, confidence 0.90

- No declaration was found stating Theorem 4.6 as a whole. Most of its content is separate lemmas about a nominal profile W and modulation v. exists_weighted_profile bundles only parts of (iii)–(iv). ProfileData/actualProfile fix one profile, keeping witnesses, the nominal cone certificate and open-annulus cone.
- Paper: “There exist fixed h ∈ (0,1/100), λ > 0, C > 1, and 0 < X_a < X_b, together with profiles E, U, Π”
- Lean: NavierStokes.LeadingStressWeights.exists_weighted_profile (NavierStokes/LeadingStressWeights.lean:1154): ∃ F W d v, FullTrueCone v ∧ WeightedBounds (lower bound and derivative bounds of the log stress against the flat weight) ∧ an inner and an outer EdgeFactor with positive angular coefficient and HasTrueDirectionCollar. Nothing from (i), (ii), (v), (vi); no uniform κ; no edge identities. Not referenced anywhere else in the repository.; NavierStokes.FinalSlowBase.ProfileData (NavierStokes/FinalSlowBase.lean:619): Structure with fields outgoing, nominal, certificate : NominalConeAssembly.Certificate nominal, loop, modulation, fullTrueCone : FullTrueCone modulation. Sub-clauses not stated for the final profile include the edge-direction identities (model edge stresses only), a uniform κ in (4.26) (pointwise cone and collar margins instead), n smooth on the closed annulus (continuous; smooth on collars), C > 1, and the moment identities (nominal profile only). - Review: Wording corrected after an independent checker called it overstated and an adjudicator re-read the cited declarations.

<a id="f4"></a>**F4. Lemma 4.9: CONTENT (medium)**, confidence 0.88

- The lemma is a conditional about any leading field that is regular at the axis, agrees with the family beyond X_tail and satisfies (4.28) and (4.25). Lean proves the clauses only for the family's own forward stress and for the specific constructed field W (and v).

- Lean: forward_stresses_eq_profile (TerminalHistoryBridge.lean:1401) and physical_forward*_eq_terminal (L1216, L1246) are about forwardTheta/forwardAxial of F. The regular-field versions, nominal_terminal_stress (LeadingStressWeights.lean:321), nominal_terminal_shears (L643) and Witness.terminal_true (NominalConeAssembly.lean:946), take the concrete W : NominalProfile.Witness F (NominalProfile.lean:2481). That is a construction, not a hypothesis class. FiveMomentCertificate is never used to derive the stress.
- Witness: Take any other regular inner extension meeting the paper's hypotheses. No declaration gives its stress as the backward integrals, its vanishing beyond X_b, its cone or (4.32).
- Consequence: The paper's argument for a general admissible field was not checked. The instance Theorem 4.6 uses (the constructed nominal and modulated field) was checked, so the main result is unaffected.
- Review: An independent checker tried to refute this finding and upheld it as written.

<a id="f5"></a>**F5. Definition 6.4: CONTENT (medium)**, confidence 0.80, contested

- The Lean mean class MeanClass states only smoothness on the open strip and the (6.24) bounds, so alone it does not imply torus periodicity. Periodicity and vanishing off the shell are a separate predicate, MovingField; band-chart compatibility is StateOn. No single predicate bundles them.

- Lean: NavierStokes.WeightedClasses.MeanClass (NavierStokes/WeightedClasses.lean:115): abbrev MeanClass s α f := MemClass s (fun _ x => s.zeta x) α f; it adds nothing to MemClass beyond fixing the weight ζ.; NavierStokes.WeightedClasses.MemClass (NavierStokes/WeightedClasses.lean:107): Three fields: weight_nonneg; smooth (ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ (f n) s.domain); bounds (for each m, some C ≥ 0 and p with ‖iteratedFDeriv j (f n) x‖ ≤ C ε_n^α (slow n · max 1 δ⁻¹)^p · w n x on the domain for j ≤ m). No periodicity, support or chart clause; StripData (:31) has no torus data.; NavierStokes.GaugeMomentBalances.MovingField (NavierStokes/GaugeMomentBalances.lean:461): For each band n: f n is smooth on slowDomain U.carrier (all radii), is SupportedGauge a b (nonzero only for radius in the closed shell [ℓ·a, ℓ·b]), and is PeriodicOn U.carrier.; NavierStokes.PhysicalMeanDomain.PeriodicOn (NavierStokes/PhysicalMeanDomain.lean:71): ∀ r s, s ∈ U → FourierAlias.TorusPeriodic (fun Y => f (r,(s,Y))), i.e. invariance under integer translations of the plane variable Y.; NavierStokes.PhysicalResidualNaturality.StateOn (NavierStokes/PhysicalResidualNaturality.lean:844): On U, each field of state s at band n (mean, pressure, oscillation, errors) equals a scaled pullback along the linear equivalence e of state r's field at band nr. It is a whole-state identity; equivalence with (6.20) was not traced.; NavierStokes.ActualInitialization.initial_on_overlap (NavierStokes/ActualInitialization.lean:674): StateOn for initialState against itself on slowDomain (overlap n m) with bandChartEquiv, when index n + k = index m. <a id="f6"></a>**F6. Lemma 8.6: CONTENT (medium)**, confidence 0.93

- Lean's torus-inverse estimate needs m+5 input derivatives; the paper's (8.19) states m+4. The Lean bound is strictly weaker by one derivative and no m+4 version exists in the repository. The paper's m+4 is correct.
- Paper: “‖N^{-1}F‖_{C^m_y} ≤ C_m ‖F‖_{C^{m+4}_y}”
- Lean: NavierStokes.SmoothFamilyTorusInverse.inverse_finiteJets (SmoothFamilyTorusInverse.lean:865): `JetBound f S (n + 5) C → JetBound (inverse d f) S n (K * C)`; ParametricTorusInverse.mixedJet_inverse_bound (:654): `xJet (w.length + 5)`; UniformFourierAlias.realInverse_finiteJets (:611): `∀ j ≤ m + 5`. Origin: applyMultiplier_finiteJets (:847) loses n + l + 4 with l = 1, because coefficient decay is summed against Σ weight^{-4} (SmoothFourierData.summable_weight_inv_four :343). A search of decls.jsonl for any inverse bound with a +4 loss finds only the centering lemma nonbarPart_finiteJets (:1020), not N^{-1}.
- Witness: m = 0: Lean gives sup\|N^{-1}F\| ≤ K‖F‖_{C^5}; it does not give sup\|N^{-1}F\| ≤ K‖F‖_{C^4}. The paper's claim holds because \|v_t·k\|^{-1} ≤ C(1+\|k\|) and Σ_{k∈Z²}(1+\|k1\|+\|k2\|)^{-3} converges (4r lattice points at ℓ¹-radius r).
- Consequence: The displayed estimate in the lemma's own statement is not what was checked; the m+4 form cannot be recovered from the m+5 form. No downstream effect: the M_α class arguments need only some fixed finite loss at each order, and the paper's proof uses the estimate only that way (p96).
- Review: An independent checker tried to refute this finding and upheld it as written.

### Low (55)

<a id="f7"></a>**F7. Definition 3.2: CONTENT (low)**, confidence 0.85

- The paper (p.27) says Q_s, N_s are axis-regular in the scalar sense of Def 3.2. Lean proves them smooth only for X≠0, and Prop 4.2's residual theorem is stated only off the axis. This is a use of Def 3.2, not the definition itself.
- Paper: “so Qs , Ns are regular at the axis in the scalar-profile sense of Definition 3.2.”
- Lean: ProfileHistories.Profiles.angularLag_smoothAt (ProfileHistories.lean:609, hX : p.1 ≠ 0), axialLag_smoothAt (:614, hX : p.1 ≠ 0); LeadingStress.navierStokesResidual_tangential (LeadingStress.lean:571, hs : 0 < radialEnergy x); no other ContDiff lemma for the lags exists
- Consequence: Route to the Prop 4.2 audit. The main result is unaffected, because the final construction gets axis regularity of stresses from SmoothCoefficients.

<a id="f8"></a>**F8. Lemma 4.1: CONTENT (low)**, confidence 0.92

- Lean profiles are functions on all of R^2 that are Frechet-differentiable at (X,eta). The paper's profiles live on X>=0 (on [0,X_c]x[-1,1] with one-sided boundary derivatives). On the axis s=0 the Lean statement needs a two-sided extension of f. This changes only the formulation.
- Paper: “For a smooth profile f and any real b”
- Lean: NavierStokes.SimilarityProfile.partialT_pullback / partialZ_pullback (SimilarityProfile.lean:216, :233), hypothesis `hf : DifferentiableAt ℝ f (inner h p)` with `InnerProfile := ℝ × ℝ → ℝ` (L20); `s = p.2.1` ranges over all of ℝ
- Witness: At s=0, X=s/q=0, so the Lean statement asks for Frechet differentiability of f at (0,eta) in R^2.
- Consequence: None for the result: a smooth profile on the closed rectangle extends smoothly (Seeley/Whitney), and the Lean statement needs only differentiability at the single point, which is weaker than smoothness.

<a id="f9"></a>**F9. Theorem 4.6: CONTENT (low)**, confidence 0.80

- The edge identities n(X_a)∥(a,−b_s) and n(X_b)=(1,0) are stated only for the activation and terminal model stresses. The final-stress collar lemmas give only a positive angular coefficient and ε-margins.
- Paper: “n(X_a,η) is parallel to (a(X_a,η), −b_s(X_a,η)); at X = X_b, n(X_b,η) = (1,0) and b_s(X_b,η) = 0”
- Lean: ActiveAnnulusWeight.exists_natural_activation_factor_aligned (ActiveAnnulusWeight.lean:1039); TerminalEdgeFactor.profileTilt_zero (TerminalEdgeFactor.lean:1333); LeadingStressWeights.inner_direction_positive (l.946) and outer_direction_positive (l.968) omit both identities
- Consequence: Not stated for the final stress. Recoverable through the chart equalities the proofs use, plus identifying referenceP1/P2 with a, −b_s at X_a. b_s(X_b)=0 follows from U=0 on [X_v,∞).

<a id="f10"></a>**F10. Lemma 4.7: CONTENT (low)**, confidence 0.80

- The family clause needs every hypothesis (nonnegativity, support, separation) on an open U containing K. A family given only on the compact set [-1,1], smooth up to the endpoints, is not covered and may have no admissible extension.
- Paper: “Its inverse and every fixed parameter derivative are bounded for a smooth compact family preserving these hypotheses.”
- Lean: SmoothPowerMomentMatrix.Family.compact_inverse_jet_bound, SmoothPowerMomentMatrix.lean:184 (also compact_inverse_jets_bound L197): (hU : IsOpen U) (hKU : K ⊆ U); Family fields bump_nonnegative, bump_support, separated hold ∀ p ∈ U (L93-111); integral_contDiffOn_of_moving_support (L26) also needs IsOpen U.
- Witness: m=1, β(η,t)=φ(t)+(1−η)ψ(t) on η∈[-1,1], with ψ≥0 supported where φ=0. Any C^1 extension past η=1 has ∂_ηβ=−ψ<0 where β(1,·)=0, so it turns negative. No open U ⊇ [-1,1] carries a Family.
- Consequence: Lean covers families that are smooth on a neighbourhood of the parameter set. Closed-interval families need a separate routine argument that is not formalized: one-sided smoothness of B, pointwise det ≠ 0, compactness. The paper's uses (pp.42–43, 140, 162) have fixed bumps and exponents. Their η-dependence comes through positive factors like K(η)=c(1+η²)^{-1}, which extend to ℝ. No Lean file consumes the family clause.

<a id="f11"></a>**F11. Lemma 4.8: CONTENT (low)**, confidence 0.85

- No Lean conclusion asserts P_* > e^{T_d} or h < e^{-T_d}. They appear only as hypotheses of exists_ordered_profile_cone and inside exists_prepared's proof. PreparedProfile exports only 2 ≤ P and h ≤ 1/1000.

<a id="f12"></a>**F12. Lemma 4.9: CONTENT (low)**, confidence 0.70

- The backward-integral formulas are proved only where the heat switch is complete (strictly X > e^{1/2}X_tail). Lemma 4.9 displays them without a radial restriction, and they are not stated directly for the regular field's stress.

TerminalStress.forward_eq_backward_iff (TerminalStress.lean:623) is abstract and is never applied to the field on all r > 0.

The cone and (4.32) are only needed on e^{1/2}X_tail ≤ X, forward_stresses_eq_profile covers the closed endpoint by continuity, and Lemma A.8 (p.140) itself restricts to y ≥ 1/2. Getting the formulas for W's stress takes composing nominal_terminal_stress with physical_forward*_eq_terminal.

<a id="f35"></a>**F35. Proposition 7.2: CONTENT (low)**, confidence 0.80

- Uniqueness is proved only among solutions of the projected ODE (7.13), with two-sided derivatives even at the endpoints. Uniqueness among pairs (t_m, π_m) with tangency, zero data and (7.5) is not stated. The converse step is routine.

- Lean: CommonCoverSolve.LinearData.anchoredSolve_unique (CommonCoverSolve.lean:472): hypothesis HasDerivAt u (coefficientAlong·u + forcingAlong) on Icc a b. No declaration derives (7.13) from (7.5) plus n·t=0.

- Consequence: This is a formal gap only. The paper's uniqueness class is reached by a three-line argument that is not formalized.

<a id="f42"></a>**F42. Corollary 7.8: CONTENT (low)**, confidence 0.70

- The conclusion class M_γ is MemClass: smoothness on s.domain plus ζ-weighted all-order bounds. It omits Definition 6.4's descent to each common torus and its explicit smooth zero extension outside the shell. This is cross-cutting with Def 6.4.

<a id="f55"></a>**F55. Lemma A.1: CONTENT (low)**, confidence 0.86

- Clause (iii) is stated on an open U ⊇ K. Smoothness and every bump hypothesis (nonnegativity, support, separation) must hold on all of U. Data smooth only on a compact K with one-sided derivatives is not literally covered.
- Paper: “If these data vary smoothly over a compact parameter set and the stated separations persist, the inverse and each fixed parameter derivative of it are …”
- Lean: NavierStokes.SmoothPowerMomentMatrix.Family (SmoothPowerMomentMatrix.lean:93) fields bump_nonnegative/bump_support: ∀ p ∈ U; compact_inverse_jets_bound (L197) needs (hU : IsOpen U) (hKU : K ⊆ U); same in SmoothExponentialMomentMatrix.Family (L22) / L98
- Witness: K=[0,1]⊂ℝ, one bump block: β(p,t)=ψ₁(t)+p·ψ₂(t), with ψ₁,ψ₂ ≥ 0 smooth in the same interval and ψ₂>0 where ψ₁=0. β ≥ 0 and β ≠ 0 on K, but every smooth extension is negative somewhere for small p<0, so no Family exists on any open U ⊇ K.
- Consequence: For a family given only on K = [0,1] (or the paper's η ∈ [−1,1]) whose bump has no nonnegative smooth extension, the Family theorem cannot be applied directly. The conclusion still follows from the file's general lemmas plus a smooth extension, so the result is not wrong. The paper's applications use fixed bumps, and the parametric Family theorems are not used downstream in the repository.

<a id="f56"></a>**F56. Lemma A.9: CONTENT (low)**, confidence 0.92

- The paper assumes b is smooth on the closed rectangle [0,δ0]×K. Lean assumes b is ContDiffOn ∞ on an open set U containing K×[0,δ0]. These agree under the usual convention, or by Seeley/Whitney extension when smoothness only holds up to the boundary.

- Consequence: Not a defect. If b is smooth only up to the boundary (one-sided derivatives at u=0 or at the ends of K), a smooth extension to a neighbourhood is needed before the Lean theorem applies. Seeley's extension theorem supplies one. The integrand only uses u∈(0,δ], and the paper's applications build b from profiles that are smooth near the collar.

No edge collars, moments, patches or analyticity, so it does not state Theorem 4.6 either.
- Consequence: Chiefly packaging; Theorem 4.6 records the same gap. (C.18), (C.19) and the support clause are stated as in the paper for every witness with FullTrueCone. Not everything conjoins directly: the edge-direction identities and a single κ were not found for the modulated stress (separate low findings), and the full set of vanishing moments only for the nominal profile. Not every clause was traced. We found nothing suggesting an error.
- Review: Wording corrected after an independent checker called it overstated and an adjudicator re-read the cited declarations. Severity: judge medium, checker low, adjudicator low; low by two of three. Adjudicator: Nothing stated is weaker than the paper: (C.18), (C.19) and the support clause hold for every witness with the cone, with no extra hypothesis and not for one instance only. The sub-clauses lacking a statement for the modulated profile are separate findings; what this finding adds is only that the clauses are not conjoined in one declaration, which the brief classes as packaging.

<a id="f61"></a>**F61. Proposition C.3: COVERAGE (low)**, confidence 0.82

- The edge identities n(X_a) ∥ (a,−b_s) and n(X_b) = (1,0) are stated by no declaration about the modulated stress. The collar lemmas hide the coefficient behind ∃ G and export only positivity and ε-margins.
- Paper: “For each η, n(X_a,η) is parallel to (a(X_a,η),−b_s(X_a,η)); at X=X_b, n(X_b,η)=(1,0)”
- Lean: LeadingStressWeights.inner_direction_positive (LeadingStressWeights.lean:946) and outer_direction_positive (:968) conclude ∃ G, 0 < (G.coefficient (η,0)).1 ∧ HasTrueDirectionCollar; HasStrictDirectionCollar (ActiveAnnulusWeight.lean:853) records no value of tilt (B (η,0)). The alignment is the halign hypothesis of EdgeFactor.aligned_collar (:806) and the change step of terminal_strict_direction (:1201)
- Consequence: Stated only for the model stresses. Recovery: modulated_activation_stress (:278) and modulated_terminal_stress (:410) equate v's stress with the models on each collar; exists_natural_activation_factor_aligned (ActiveAnnulusWeight.lean:1039) with modulated_activation_shears (:610) and actual_shear_coordinates_zero (:917), and TerminalEdgeFactor.profileStressFactor_zero (:1277), then give both directions. Downstream uses only the margins.

## Structural notes

From the repository atlas (`work/atlas-result.json`, `arch.notes`):

The "table" on pp. 14-15 is the pair of flowcharts Figures 5 (p13) and 6 (p15).

Structural findings:
1. The paper's intermediate results have no separate Lean statements. No declaration states Theorem 3.1, Proposition 9.9, Proposition 10.1 or Lemmas 10.2/10.3 on their own. They are combined inside GermCandidateAssembly.exists_candidate_witness_of_finite_stages (GermCandidateAssembly.lean:164), whose output is a periodic candidate (ProblemStatement.CandidateProperties, ProblemStatement.lean:101). Theorem 3.1(iii) is restated as a joint-jet limit at (1,0) (VanishingJointJets). Theorem 3.1(ii) is restated as AwayExtensions.
2. The R3 route goes through a periodic step. The paper builds the R3 fields and force directly (Prop 10.1, Lemma 10.3). Lean periodizes and time-activates the cut sums, builds a Borel force for the periodized residual, and then cuts back to R3. The cut-back uses R3CompactCandidate.of_localized_fields, an outer force cutoff (compactForce) and a time cutoff PositiveTimeForce.force; neither cutoff appears in the paper. Corollary 10.6 then periodizes the R3 candidate again, which is the paper's route. A native periodic adapter (option_D_of_candidate) exists but is unused.
3. The blowup is shown differently. Lean uses the axial velocity at the origin, u(t,0) = (1-t)^{-A} j e_z (FinalSlowBase.origin, FinalSlowBase.lean:361). The paper uses the swirl path of Theorem 3.1(iv)/(3.6). Nothing on the main path states (3.6).
4. Lemma 10.4 on the main path is a weaker Gronwall bound. The sharp F(t)^2 inequality is proved (candidate_energy_estimates) but feeds only theorem_1_1_with_dissipation. The maximal-lifespan and H3 claims of p124 exist only for the periodic step (CandidateConsequences.Consequences) and are unused.
5. Steps that exist only in Lean: the comparator adapters, the initial-rest clause |t| <= 3/8 (needed for the compression), and the fixed support cylinder (radius 1/4, height 1/2) in place of the paper's q*-dependent cutoff.
6. The decay exponent sigma_j = 1/5 + j/10 appears only as an abstract gain sequence tending to infinity (StageEstimates.gain).
7. Docstrings cite a different numbering ("Proposition 11.4", "Lemma 11.3", "Proposition 11.7", "Lemma 3.3", "Proposition 5.1"), which does not match this manuscript. Treat docstring cross-references as unreliable.
8. Profile and wave mappings (Section 3.2, Section 3.3) rest only on module docs and were not checked item by item.

Mechanical checks (a comment- and string-aware scan of all 2659 `.lean` files at the pinned commit):

- `NavierStokes/`: 0 `sorry`, 0 `admit`, 0 `axiom` declarations, 0 `native_decide`.
- `sorry` anywhere: 4 occurrences, all in `ComparatorChallenges/Euler.lean`, `ComparatorChallenges/NavierStokes.lean` (Comparator's challenge placeholders).
- Comparator's permitted axioms (`ComparatorChallenges/NavierStokes.json`): `propext`, `Quot.sound`, `Classical.choice`.
